%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 1: CHANDRAGRAHAṆAVIKĀRAḤ (Lunar Eclipse Chapter)
%% ========================================================================
\section{\dev{चन्द्रग्रहणविकारः} — Lunar Eclipse Variations}
\label{sec:chandra-grahana}

\thispagestyle{fancy}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

\subsection*{Introduction}

This chapter (\dev{चन्द्रग्रहणविकारः}) deals with the computation of lunar eclipses,
following Brahmagupta's exposition in the \dev{ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्त}. The commentary
(\dev{सुधाकर-टीका}) by Paṇḍita Kṛpāludatta Sūnu Sudhākara Dvivedī provides
elaborate explanations in Hindi, with mathematical demonstrations (\dev{उपपत्ति}).

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

\subsection{Verses 11--12: Computation of \dev{इष्टग्रास} (Desired Eclipse Magnitude)}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
इष्टन्यूनस्थितिदलगुणा भुक्तिविल्लेपलिप्ता पश्चादष्टा भवति हि भुजः कोटिरिष्टेणुवाणः \\
तद्वेकोद्भवमपि पदं कर्णं एतेन हीनं मानैक्यार्धं स्फुटमिह भवेदाकलित छन्नमानम्॥ ११--१२ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
इष्टन्यूनस्थितिदलगुणा भुक्तिविल्लेपलिप्ता पश्चादष्टा भवति हि भुजः कोटिरिष्टेणुवाणः।
तद्वेकोद्भवमपि पदं कर्णं एतेन हीनं मानैक्यार्धं स्फुटमिह भवेदाकलित छन्नमानम्॥
\end{sanskritverse}

The verse gives the method for computing the \dev{इष्टग्रास} (desired eclipse magnitude).
From the \dev{स्थितिदल} (half-duration) diminished by the desired quantity, multiplied
by the \dev{भुक्ति} (daily motion) and divided by the \dev{विल्लेप} (deflection), one gets
the \dev{भुज} (sine). The \dev{कोटि} (cosine) is computed by the rule of \dev{इषटेनु} (desired quantity).
From the \dev{मानैक्यार्ध} (half-sum of diameters), the \dev{कर्ण} (hypotenuse) being subtracted,
the result gives the true obscuration.

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
इष्ट रहित स्थितिदल को भुक्ति से गुणा कर विल्लेप से भाग देने से भुज होता है, इस फल करके
सूर्य, चन्द्र और पात को चालन देकर तात्कालिक चन्द्रशर साधन करना वह कोटि है। उन दोनों
(भुज और कोटि) का वर्गयोग मूल कर्ण होता है, मानैक्यार्ध (छाद्य और छादक बिम्ब के व्यासार्ध
योग) में से कर्ण को घटाने से जो शेष रहता है वह इष्टग्रास होता है इति॥ ११--१२ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
स्पर्शी के बाद जितनी इष्ट घटी में ग्रास-ज्ञान अपेक्षित हो उस इष्ट घटी को स्थित्यर्ध में से घटा
देना, शेष से अनुपात करते हैं। यदि साठ घटी में रवि और चन्द्र की गत्यन्तर कला पाते हैं तो
इष्टोन स्थित्यर्ध घटी में क्या इससे जो फल होता है वह भुज है, अनुपातागत फल से चालित रवि,
चन्द्र और पात से तात्कालिक चन्द्रवार साधन करना वह कोटि है। इन दोनों (भुज और कोटि)
का वर्गयोग मूल कर्ण होता है, मानैक्यार्ध में से कर्ण को घटाने से इष्ट ग्रास होता है।
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verses 13--14: Computation of \dev{इष्टग्रासात्काल} (Time from Desired Magnitude)}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्रसकृत्-ग्रासकलोनप्रमाणयुतिदलकृते विशोध्य कृतिम्।
तात्कालिकविक्षेपस्य शेषमूलं कृतं तिथिवत्॥ १३ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
प्रग्रहणस्थित्यर्धात् प्रोह्य प्रग्रहणतो भवेत् कालः।
सौक्षं विशोष्य मोक्षस्थित्यर्धात् प्राग् भवेन्मोक्षात्॥ १४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
ग्रासकलोनप्रमाणयुतिदलकृते ग्रासोनमानैक्यार्धवर्गात् तात्कालिकशरस्य कृति विद्धोध्य
शेषशूलं तिथिवदसकृत् कृतम्। अर्थात् शेषमूलं षष्टिगुणां रविचन्द्रगत्यन्तरहतं फलकालेन
रविचन्द्रपातानु प्रचाल्य तात्कालिकविक्षेपं संसाध्य तस्मात् पुनः ग्रासिकलोनप्रमाणेत्यादिना
असकृत् शेषमूलकालः स्थिरीभवति तं प्रग्रहणस्थित्यर्धात् (स्पर्शिकस्थित्यर्धात्) प्रोह्य
(हित्वा) प्रग्रहणतः (स्पर्शादनन्तरं) कालो भवेत्।
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
ग्रासकलोनप्रमाणयुतिदलकृतेः (ग्रासरहितमानैक्यार्धवर्गात्) तात्कालिकशरस्य कृति
(वर्ग) विशोध्य (हित्वा) शेषस्थ मूल तिथिवदसकृत् कृतमर्थात् शेषमूलं षष्टिगुणं
रविचन्द्रयोगंत्यन्तरभक्तं फलकालेन रविचन्द्रपातानु प्रचाल्य तात्कालिकचन्द्रशरं संसाध्य
तस्मात् पुनर्ग्रासकलोनप्रमाणयुतिदलकृतेरित्यादिना असकृत् शेषमूलं कालः स्थिरीभवति
ते प्रग्रहणस्थित्यर्धात् (स्पर्शिकस्थित्यर्धात्) प्रोह्य (हित्वा) प्रग्रहणतः (स्पर्शादनन्तरं)
कालो भवेदर्थात् स्पर्शानन्तरमेतावतीष्ठकाले तावानिष्टग्रासो भवति॥ १३--१४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
तत्कालशरस्याज्ञानान्मध्यकालिकशरात् कर्म कृतमतोऽसकृद्धिघिना स्फुटकालसाधनमुचितम्।
शेषोपपत्ति ``ग्रसोन मानैक्यदलस्य वर्गाद् विक्षेपकृत्या रहिता'' इत्यादिभास्करविधिना
स्फुटा ॥ १३--१४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 9: Sphuṭīkaraṇa of \dev{स्थितिविमर्दार्ध}}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
षष्ट्या विभाजिता स्थितिविमर्दार्धनाडिकागुणा स्वगतिः।
छाद्यो रवीन्दुपातेष्वुभयसकृत् तैर्धनमन्ते॥ ९ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
स्वगतिः (रविगतिः, चन्द्रगतिः, पातगतिश्च) स्थितिविमर्दार्धनाडिका गुणा
(स्थित्यर्ध घटीमिविमर्दार्ध घटीभिः पृथक् पृथक् गुणिता) षष्ट्या विभाजिता छाद्ये
रविचन्द्रपातेषु उभयसकृत् तैर्धनमन्ते।
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
``स्थित्यर्धनाडीगुणिता स्वमुक्ति'' इत्यादिना एवं विमर्दार्धफलोनयुक्त इत्यादिना च
भास्करविधिना स्फुटा ॥ ९ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 2: SŪRYAGRAHAṆĀDHIKĀRAḤ (Solar Eclipse Chapter)
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section{\dev{सूर्यग्रहणाधिकारः} — Solar Eclipse Chapter}
\label{sec:surya-grahana}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

This chapter (\dev{सूर्यग्रहणाधिकारः}, the fifth chapter of the text) deals with the
computation of solar eclipses. The chapter begins with the statement of purpose
(\dev{प्रयोजन}) and proceeds through the computation of the five \dev{ज्या} (sines)
required for eclipse calculation: \dev{उदयज्या}, \dev{मध्यज्या}, \dev{रविदयाकर्ण},
\dev{विशिमशङ्कु}, and \dev{हृक्षेप}.

\subsection{Verse 1: Statement of Purpose}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
अथ सूर्यग्रहणाधिकारः प्रारभ्यते। तत्रादौ तदारम्भप्रयोजनमाह—
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
ग्रहभास्करान्तरं प्राक् पद बाधकं ग्रहान्तरे यस्मात्।
स्वांशैर्यथाह्यास्तस्माद् द्युते तदानयनम्॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राक् पूर्वे क्षितिजे प्रभास्करान्तरैः स्वांशैः स्वकालांशैः पश्चात् पश्चिमक्षितिजे
ग्रहास्तरैः स्वकालांशैर्यथासंख्यं ग्रहाः हश्याहश्याः भवन्ति। तस्मात् तदानयनं
प्रहर्कान्तरानयनं वक्ष्ये इति। अर्थात् प्राक्-क्षितिजे यस्मिन् दिने ग्रहोदयादनन्तरं
कालांशघटीभिः रवेरुदयस्तस्मिन् दिने ग्रहो रात्रिशेषे हश्यो भवति। एवं यस्मिन् दिने
पश्चिमक्षितिजे रव्यस्तानन्तरं कालांशघटीमित्रं हस्यास्तमयस्तस्मिन् दिने सायंकाले
ग्रहस्य हश्यत्वम्॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
जिस कारण से सूर्यग्रहण में पञ्चज्या (उदयज्या, मध्यज्या, रविदयाकर्ण, विशिमशङ्कु,
हृक्षेप) से भास्करादि आचार्यों ने जो प्रानयन किया है उससे गणितैक्य (वेधोपलब्ध और
गणितागत विषयों की तुल्यता) नहीं होता है उस कारण से जैसे उनका ऐक्य (वेधोपलब्ध और
गणितागत विषयों के समत्व) हो वैसे मैं प्रानयन को कहता हूँ। तिथ्यन्ते इसका ग्रहे से
सम्बन्ध है, उदयज्या -- भ्रमा, मध्यज्या -- दशमलग्ननतांशज्या, विशिमशङ्कु -- हग्गति,
हृक्षेप -- विशिमनतांशज्या, सूर्यसिद्धान्त में भी पूर्वोक्त पञ्चज्या ही से सूर्यग्रहण का
प्रानयन है, पौलिशसिद्धान्त में सूर्यग्रहण में चन्द्र की पृथक् पञ्चज्या साधित है इसलिए
वहाँ द्विज्या विधान से सूर्यग्रहण उपनिबद्ध है इति ॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verses 2--3: Conditions for \dev{लम्बन} (Deflection) and \dev{अवनति} (Depression)}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
विश्रिमलग्नसमं एकं न लम्बनं तदधिकोनके भवति।
तस्य क्रान्तिज्योदक् यदा अक्षनोवा समा न तदा॥ २ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
अवनतिरतोऽन्यथा भवति सम्भवे तदुदयैव लग्नसमम्।
तदुदितघटिकास्तच्छङ्कुस्तच्चरप्राणैः परे॥ ३ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
तिथ्यन्ते गणितागतदर्शान्तिकाले पूर्वविधिना त्रिप्रश्नोक्तेन लग्नं कृत्वा विश्रिमं
कार्यम्। तेन विश्रिमलग्नेन समेकं रवौ सति लम्बनं न भवति! तस्माद् विश्रिमादधिके
कोने रवौ लम्बनं भवतीत्यर्थत् एव सिध्यति। एवं तस्य विश्रिमस्योत्तरा क्रान्तिज्या यदा
स्वदेशाक्षजोवा समा तदा अवनतिर्न भवति अतोऽन्यथा चावनतिर्भवति।
लम्बनावनत्योः सम्भवे च तदुदयं स्वदेशीयराश्युदयैषि-लग्नसमं कृत्वा
आद्यूनस्य भोग्योद्धिकभुक्तियुक्तो मध्योदयाद्य इति त्रिप्रश्नोक्तविधिं कृत्वा
तदुदितघटिकास्तस्य विश्रिमस्य गता घटिकाः साध्याः॥ २--३ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 7: \dev{स्पष्टदर्शान्तकालिक} Computation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
घटिकामिरगुणिताः पञ्चभिर्भक्ता लब्धकलामियंति लम्बनम्।
तदा गणितागतदर्शान्तकालिका रविचन्द्रपाता ऊनाः कार्याः॥ ७ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
घटिकामिरगुणिताः पञ्चभ्यः पञ्चज्या भक्ता लब्धकलामियंति लम्बनं तदा
गणितागतदर्शान्तकालिका रविचन्द्रपाता ऊनाः (हीनाः) कार्याः, यदि लम्बनं धनं तदा
लब्धकलाभिर्गणितदर्शान्तकालिका रविचन्द्रपाता युक्ताः कार्यास्तदा स्पष्टदर्शान्तकालिका
रविचन्द्रपाता भवन्तीति ॥ ७ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
गणितागतदर्शान्तकालिकरविचन्द्रपातेभ्यः शरादिकं सर्वमानेतव्यम्। ऋणात्मके
लम्बने ``पञ्चषष्टिकामिरः पृथक् पृथक् रविचन्द्रपातगतिकला लम्यन्ते तदा
लम्बनघटिकाभिः किमित्यनुपातेन'' लब्धकलाभिर्गणितागतदर्शान्तकालिका
रविचन्द्रपाता हीनााः कार्याः, धनात्मके लम्बने लब्धकलाभिस्ते युक्तास्तदा ते
स्पष्टदर्शान्तकालिका भवेयुरेवेति, सिद्धान्तशेखरे ``गतिहतां लम्बननाडिकामिर्विभज्य
पञ्चया फललिप्तिकामिः। रवीन्दुपाताः सहिता धनाख्ये विवर्जिताश्च क्षयलम्बने ते''
इत्यनेन श्रीपतिनाचार्योक्तानुरूपमेवोक्तमिति ॥ ७ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}


%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 3: CHANDRACCHĀYĀDHIKĀRAḤ (Moon's Shadow Chapter)
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section{\dev{चन्द्रच्छायाधिकारः} — Moon's Shadow Chapter}
\label{sec:chandra-chhaya}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

This chapter (\dev{चन्द्रच्छायाधिकारः}) deals with the computation of the Moon's
shadow, which is essential for understanding lunar phenomena. The shadow of the
Earth falls on the Moon during a lunar eclipse, and its computation involves
calculating the \dev{च्छाया} (shadow), \dev{मध्यच्छाया} (middle shadow), and related
quantities.

\subsection{Verse 1: Beginning of the Chapter}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
अथ चन्द्रच्छायाधिकारः प्रारभ्यते। तत्रादौ तदारम्भप्रयोजनमाह—
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राक् चन्द्रलग्नयोरन्तरं सप्तलग्नादीनां यतस्ततं पश्चात्।
प्रतिदिनमिन्दोश्च्छाया यतस्तदनयनमभिचार्ये॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राक् (पूर्वस्थितिजे) चन्द्रलग्नयोरन्तरं, पश्चात् (पश्चिमस्थितिजे) सप्तलग्नचन्द्रयोः
अन्तरं च प्रतिदिनं यतनेन यतः (यस्मात्कारणात्) इन्दुच्छाया (चन्द्रच्छाया) ज्ञायते,
तथेन्दुच्छायातस्तदनन्तरं च ज्ञायते ततस्तदानयन (छायानयन) मभिचार्ये (कथयिष्ये) इति।
सिद्धान्तशेखरे ``प्रायतस्तु हीनरविमलग्नयोरन्तरलग्ननाशिनोश्च पश्चिमे'' इत्यादि
इनेन श्रीपतिनाचार्योक्तानुरूपमेवोक्तमिति ॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
अब चन्द्रच्छायाधिकार प्रारम्भ किया जाता है, पहले उसके प्रारम्भ-प्रयोजन को कहते हैं।
पूर्व स्थितिजे में चन्द्र और लग्न का अन्तर, पश्चिम स्थितिजे में सप्तलग्न और चन्द्र का अन्तर
प्रत्येक दिन में जो होता है उसी से क्योंकि चन्द्रच्छाया समझी जाती है (प्रथम चन्द्रच्छाया
का ज्ञान होता है) तथा चन्द्रच्छाया से उस अन्तर का ज्ञान होता है इसलिये छायानयन को
कहता हूँ॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 2: Shadow Computation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्रभहुण्डाकृतिकोर्ध्वकालिकोद्बलनखण्डसमस्थितिपाताः।
छन्दोव्यासस्तलग्ने स्वभग्रप्रायाण्न सङ्क्षेपः॥ २ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्रभहुण्डाकृतिकोर्ध्वकालिकोद्बलनखण्डसमस्थितिपाताः छन्दोव्यासस्तलग्ने
स्वभग्रप्रायाण्न सङ्क्षेपः॥ २ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verses 3--4: Detailed Shadow Calculation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
इदानीं कर्तव्यतामाह—
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राग्रहुण्डाकृतिकोर्ध्वकालिकोद्बलनखण्डसमस्थितिपाताः।
छन्दोव्यासस्तलग्ने स्वभग्रप्रायाण्न सङ्क्षेपः॥ ३ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
मूलोनं मूलं गतं यद्वदुद्धृत्यागतं ततोऽन्यत्।
स्वभुक्त्या हृतमभीष्टच्छायागतिः सैव मूलोद्धृता॥ ४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 4: GRAHODAYĀSTĀDHIKĀRAḤ (Planetary Rising/Setting Chapter)
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section{\dev{ग्रहोदयास्ताधिकारः} — Planetary Rising and Setting}
\label{sec:grahodayasta}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

This chapter (\dev{ग्रहोदयास्ताधिकारः}) deals with the computation of the rising
and setting times of planets. The chapter explains how to determine the times
when planets become visible (\dev{उदय}) and invisible (\dev{अस्त}) based on their
elongation from the Sun.

\subsection{Verse 1: Pratyaya (Introduction)}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
अथोदयास्ताधिकारः प्रारभ्यते। तत्रादौ तदारम्भप्रयोजनमाह—
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
ग्रहभास्करान्तरं प्राक् पद बाधकं ग्रहान्तरे यस्मात्।
स्वांशैर्यथाह्यास्तस्माद् द्युते तदानयनम्॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राक् पूर्वे क्षितिजे प्रभास्करान्तरैः स्वांशैः स्वकालांशैः पश्चात् पश्चिमक्षितिजे
ग्रहास्तरैः स्वकालांशैर्यथासंख्यं ग्रहाः हश्याहश्याः भवन्ति। तस्मात् तदानयनं
प्रहर्कान्तरानयनं वक्ष्य इति। अर्थात् प्राक्-क्षितिजे यस्मिन् दिने ग्रहोदयादनन्तरं
कालांशघटीभिः रवेरुदयस्तस्मिन् दिने प्रभो रात्रिशेषे हश्यो भवति। एवं यस्मिन् दिने
पश्चिमक्षितिजे रव्यस्तानन्तरं कालांशघटीमित्रं हस्यास्तमयस्तस्मिन् दिने सायंकाले
ग्रहस्य हश्यत्वम्॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verses 8--9: Daily Rising and Setting}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}

The computation of daily rising and setting of planets is described through
iterative methods (\dev{असकृत्-कर्म}) applied to the planetary positions.

\begin{sanskritverse}
तात्कालिक ग्रह लग्न से असकृत् कर्म द्वारा प्रतिदिन उदय और अस्त साधन करना
प्रथम जिस समय ग्रहोदित घटी ज्ञात करना हो उस समय के लग्न (तात्कालिक लग्न)
तथा ग्रहोदय लग्न साधन कर दोनों के अन्तरघटी साधन कर उन (साधित घटी) से ग्रह
को चलाकर उसके फिर उदयलग्न साधन कर तात्कालिक स्थिर लग्न से पुनः इष्टघटी
साधन करना, इसतरह असकृत् (वारंवार) करना॥ ८ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 9: Moon's Rising and Setting Computation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
उदयास्तमयाविन्दोः कालांशैरंकसंमितैः कार्यः।
हीनत्वं त्वधिकत्वं तदन्तरे योगकालः स्यात्॥ ९ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
अंकसंमितैः कालांशैररात् द्वादशकालांशैरिन्दोश्चन्द्रस्योदयास्तमयौ साध्यौ।
अत्र पठितकालांशेन इष्टकालांशातां यदि हीनत्वं वाधिकत्वं भवेत् तदा तदन्तरे तयोः
पठितकालांशयोरन्तरे योगकालो योगवत्‌ वा योगवत्‌ कालः स्यादिति सप्तमश्लोकेन
स्फुटोक्तम्॥ ९ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
अंकसंमितैः कालांशैररथात् द्वादशतुल्यैः कालांशैरुदयास्तमयौ साध्यो। पठितकालांशस्य
इष्टकालांशस्य यदि हीनत्वं (अल्पत्वं) वा अधिकत्वं भवेत् तदन्तरे तयोः कालांशयोरन्तरे
योगकालः स्यात्॥ ९ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}


%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 5: ŚUKLONNATYADHIKĀRAḤ (Moon's Brightness Chapter)
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section{\dev{शुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः} — The Brightness of the Moon}
\label{sec:shuklonnati}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

This chapter (\dev{शुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः}) deals with the computation of the
illuminated portion of the Moon's disk — the \dev{शुक्ल} (bright) portion.
The chapter explains how to determine the amount of the Moon's surface that
is illuminated by the Sun at any given time.

\subsection{Verses 1--3: Sphuṭaśuklonnati Computation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
अथ शुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः प्रारभ्यते।
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
रविचन्द्रपातलग्नैः स्वक्रान्त्युदयात् स्वलग्नगतशेषाः।
घटिकाः खचरार्धास्तात् स्वेष्टी रविशीतथू कृत्वा॥ ४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
खचरार्धास्ताद् रव्यस्तात् स्वलग्नगतशेषाः शशिन उदयलग्नस्य गताः दोषा वा
घटिकाः साध्याः। कैः। रविचन्द्रपातलग्नैस्तथा स्वक्रान्त्युदयात्। अत्रैतदुक्तं भवति।
रव्यस्तकाले रविचन्द्रः पातो लग्नम्। एतानि कृत्वा स्वक्रान्त्या चन्द्रक्रान्त्या
उदयात् चन्द्रोदयलग्नाज्ञोदयलग्नस्य गता वा दोषा घटिका ऊनस्य भोग्योद्धिकभुक्तियुक्तो
मध्योदयाद्य इत्यनेन विधिना साध्याः। सूर्यास्तानन्तरं यावतीभिः षटिकाभिश्चन्द्रास्तस्ताः
गता घटिकाः। सूर्यास्तात् प्राग् यावतीष्ठिकाभिश्चन्द्रास्तस्ता एषा घटिकाः। एवं प्राक्
क्षितिजे रव्युदयात् गता एषा वा चन्द्रोदयघटिकाः साध्या इत्यर्थादवगम्यते॥ ४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
तात्कालिक ग्रह लग्न से असकृत् कर्म द्वारा प्रतिदिन उदय और अस्त साधन करना प्रथम
जिस समय ग्रहोदित घटी ज्ञात करना हो उस समय के लग्न (तात्कालिक लग्न) तथा
ग्रहोदय लग्न साधन कर दोनों के अन्तरघटी साधन कर उन (साधित घटी) से ग्रह को
चलाकर उसके फिर उदयलग्न साधन कर तात्कालिक स्थिर लग्न से पुनः इष्टघटी साधन
करना, इसतरह असकृत् (वारंवार) करना, एवं भस्तकाल ज्ञान के लिये भस्तलग्न (सप्तभस्त
लग्न) से कर्म करना चाहिये। इस तरह प्रथम चन्द्र दर्शन दिन से पूर्णिमा पर्यन्त इष्ट
दिन में चन्द्र के सूर्यास्त से त्रा सूर्योदय से अन्तमय काल और उदयिक काल साधन करना॥ ४ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 5: The \dev{शुक्लोन्नति} (Bright Elevation)}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
रविचन्द्रपातलग्नैः स्वक्रान्त्युदयात् स्वलग्नगतशेषाः।
घटिकाः खचरार्धास्तात् स्वेष्टी रविशीतथू कृत्वा॥ ५ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
यस्मिन् दिने सूर्यास्तादनन्तरं कालांशघटीभोद्धिकाभिश्चन्द्रास्तः। रव्युदयात् प्राक्
कालांशघटीभोद्धिकाभिर्चन्द्रोदयस्तस्मिन्नेव चन्द्रदर्शनं सति द्वे श्वज्योन्नतिः साध्या।
सितशुक्लोन्नतिरच बिम्बार्धं चन्द्रसितम् एवं भास्करेण मासान्तपादे प्रथमे च
शुक्लोन्नतिः साधिता। द्वितीयतृतीयपादयोर्बिम्बार्धं कृष्णं भवति। ततस्तत्र
कृष्णशुक्लोन्नतिर्भवति। इत्याचार्येण शुक्लोन्नतिद्वयं साध्यते। तदयं पादचर्चा न
कृता। भास्करेण न हि स्पष्टा कृष्णशुक्लोन्नतिः प्रेक्ष्यते इति मनसि संप्रधार्य
सितशुक्लोन्नतिरेव साधिता। उक्तं च ``द्वितीयतृतीययोरपि चरणयोर्ब्रह्मगुप्तादयः कृष्णशुक्लोन्नतिरानीता सा मम न संमता। न हि नरे कृष्णः शुक्लोन्नतिः स्पष्टोपलक्ष्यते''॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verses 11--12: \dev{स्पष्टशुक्ल} (True Bright Portion) Computation}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
तच्छाया गुणिते वा मध्यगती तिथिगुणस्वकरं हते।
फलयोदिक् साम्येऽन्तरमवनतिरंक्यं दिगन्यत्वे॥ ११ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
हृज्ये रविचन्द्रयोः क्षेपी स्वस्वमध्यगतिगुरो तिथिगुणितव्यासदलेन पञ्चदशगुरिणा
तrijyayā bhakta hṛte pṛthak pṛthak ravi-candra-yor-natī bhavataḥ। वा तयोर्मध्यगती
तयोः क्षेपसमं हृज्ययोः छाये ताम्यां गुरिते तिथिगुणस्वस्वच्छायाकर्महते तयोरन्तरी भवतः।
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\upapatti
\begin{sanskritverse}
प्रथमानयनस्य ``सहक्षेपाघ्ना इन्दोर्निजमध्यभुक्तितिथ्यंशनिघ्ना'' इत्यादि
भास्करविधिना स्फुटा।
\end{sanskritverse}

%% ========================================================================
%% CHAPTER 6: CHANDRAŚUKLONNATYADHIKĀRAḤ (Moon's Brightness Chapter, contd.)
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section{\dev{चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः} — The Moon's Bright Elevation (Chapter VII)}
\label{sec:chandra-shuklonnati}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

This is the seventh \dev{अध्याय} (chapter) of the \dev{ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्त}, dealing with
the Moon's bright elevation (\dev{चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नति}). It comprises eighteen verses
(\dev{श्लोक}) and covers the computation of the arc of illumination on the
Moon's disk.

\subsection{Verses 1--2: Introduction}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
अथ चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः प्रारम्भः।
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
प्राग्रहुण्डाकृतिकोर्ध्वकालिकोद्बलनखण्डसमस्थितिपाताः।
छन्दोव्यासस्तलग्ने स्वभग्रप्रायाण्न सङ्क्षेपः॥ १ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
मूलोनं मूलं गतं यद्वदुद्धृत्यागतं ततोऽन्यत्।
स्वभुक्त्या हृतमभीष्टच्छायागतिः सैव मूलोद्धृता॥ २ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\subsection{Verse 18: Chapter Conclusion}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{\dev{सूत्रम् (Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
इदानीमध्यायोपसंहारमाह—
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
इति बाहुकोटिकर्णस्फुटसितपरिलेखसूत्रकर्दमेषु।
द्वाराष्टादशा चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नतिसप्तमोऽध्यायः॥ १८ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\arth
\begin{sanskritverse}
परिलेखसूत्रमेव कर्ण इति... परिलेखसूत्रकर्णः। कोषं स्पष्टार्थम्॥ १८ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\noindent\hindi
\begin{sanskritverse}
इस तरह बाहु, कोटि, कर्ण, स्फुट, सिताङ्गुल, परिलेखसूत्र (कर्ण) में अष्टादश
भावार्थश्लोकों से चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नति नामक सप्तम अध्याय है॥ १८ ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\vspace{1em}
\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

\noindent\textbf{\dev{अन्तिमश्लोकः (Concluding Verse):}}
\begin{sanskritverse}
मधुसूदनसूनुनोदितो यस्तिलकः श्रीपथुनेह जिष्णुजोक्तः।
हृदि तं विनिधाय नूतनोऽयं रचितः सूर्यगतिः सुधाकरेण॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{sanskritverse}
इति श्रीकृपालुदत्तसूनुसुधाकरद्विवेदिविरचिते ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्तनुतनतिलके
चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः सप्तमः॥ ७० ॥
\end{sanskritverse}

\begin{center}
\textcolor{rulecolor}{\rule{0.8\textwidth}{0.5pt}}
\end{center}

\noindent\textbf{॥ इति श्रीब्रह्मगुप्तविरचिते ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्ते चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः सप्तमः समाप्तः ॥}

\vspace{2cm}

%% ========================================================================
%% APPENDIX: NOTES ON THE TEXT AND COMMENTARY
%% ========================================================================
\newpage
\section*{Appendix: Notes on the Text and Commentary}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix: Notes on the Text and Commentary}

\subsection*{About the \dev{ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्त}}

The \dev{ब्राह्मस्फुटसिद्धान्त} (Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta, ``Correctly Established
Doctrine of Brahma'') is the magnum opus of the great Indian mathematician
and astronomer \textbf{Brahmagupta} (598--668 CE). Composed in 628 CE at
Bhinmal (in present-day Rajasthan, India), it consists of 24 chapters
(\dev{अध्याय}) containing a total of 1008 verses (\dev{श्लोक}) in
\dev{आर्या} and \dev{अनुष्टुभ्} meters.

\subsection*{Contents of This Chunk (Pages 281--420)}

This typeset volume covers the following chapters from Part II of the text:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{\dev{चन्द्रग्रहणविकारः}} — Lunar Eclipse Variations \\
  Deals with the computation of eclipse magnitudes, durations, and the
  \dev{इष्टग्रास} (desired obscuration) method.

\item \textbf{\dev{सूर्यग्रहणाधिकारः}} — Solar Eclipse Chapter \\
  Covers the computation of solar eclipses, including the five \dev{ज्या}
  (sines) required: \dev{उदयज्या}, \dev{मध्यज्या}, \dev{रविदयाकर्ण},
  \dev{विशिमशङ्कु}, and \dev{हृक्षेप}.

\item \textbf{\dev{चन्द्रच्छायाधिकारः}} — Moon's Shadow Chapter \\
  Treats the computation of the Earth's shadow as seen on the Moon's disk.

\item \textbf{\dev{ग्रहोदयास्ताधिकारः}} — Planetary Rising and Setting \\
  Explains the methods for computing the heliacal rising (\dev{उदय}) and
  setting (\dev{अस्त}) of planets.

\item \textbf{\dev{शुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः}} — The Brightness of the Moon \\
  Computes the illuminated portion of the Moon's disk.

\item \textbf{\dev{चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यधिकारः}} — Moon's Bright Elevation \\
  The seventh chapter, with 18 verses, covering the arc of illumination.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{The Commentary (\dev{नूतनतिलक})}

The Hindi commentary (\dev{टीका}) in this edition was composed by
\textbf{Paṇḍita Kṛpāludatta Sūnu Sudhākara Dvivedī}. The commentary follows
the traditional structure of Indian mathematical commentaries:

\begin{itemize}
\item \dev{सूत्र} (Sūtra) — The original Sanskrit verse
\item \dev{सूत्रार्थ} (Sūtrārtha) — Literal meaning of the verse (marked \dev{सु. भा.})
\item \dev{विवृति} (Vivṛti) — Detailed explanation (marked \dev{वि. भा.})
\item \dev{हिन्दी भाष्य} (Hindi Bhāṣya) — Hindi explanation (marked \dev{हि. भा.})
\item \dev{उपपत्ति} (Upapatti) — Mathematical proof or demonstration
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Mathematical Notation}

Brahmagupta's work represents one of the high points of Indian mathematics.
Key contributions in these chapters include:

\begin{itemize}
\item Rules for computing eclipse magnitudes using the \dev{भुज} (sine) and
  \dev{कोटि} (cosine)
\item The \dev{कर्ण} (hypotenuse) formula: $\text{karpa} = \sqrt{\text{bhuj}^2 + \text{koti}^2}$
\item Iterative methods (\dev{असकृत्}) for refining planetary positions
\item Proportional reasoning (\dev{त्रैराशिक}) for time conversions
\item Geometric constructions using \dev{क्षेत्र} (diagrams)
\end{itemize}

%% ========================================================================
\section{Introduction to This Text Edition}
%% ========================================================================

This document presents Chunk 4 (original pages 1259--1398) of the second part of
Brahmagupta's \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta}, one of the most important mathematical
and astronomical treatises of ancient India. Composed around 628 CE, this work
contains 24 chapters covering topics in mathematics, astronomy, and calendar
computations.

\subsection{Contents of This Chunk}

The pages in this chunk (1259--1398) contain:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
  \item \textbf{Sanskrit verses (\dev{श्लोक})} --- Original aphoristic verses in
  devanāgarī script, forming the core text of the siddhānta
  \item \textbf{Hindi commentary (\dev{टीका})} --- Detailed explanatory commentary
  in Hindi, providing step-by-step explanations of the Sanskrit verses
  \item \textbf{Mathematical derivations} --- Algebraic and geometric working in
  traditional Indian mathematical notation
  \item \textbf{Chapter on planetary motion (\dev{भग्रहृत्यधिकार})} --- Rules for
  computing the positions and motions of the planets
  \item \textbf{Index of subjects (\dev{विषय})} --- A detailed topical index
  appearing on page 1338
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Structure of the Edition}

Each original page is presented as transcribed text with its original page
number clearly indicated. The pages are grouped by content area for navigational
convenience. The original edition follows the traditional format:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
  \item Sanskrit verses in devanāgarī script, numbered sequentially
  \item Hindi commentary (headed \dev{सू. भा.} = Sūtra Bhāṣya) providing
  glosses and explanations
  \item Mathematical working shown in traditional notation
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{How to Read the Mathematical Notation}

The mathematical formulas appear in traditional Indian algebraic notation where:
\dev{य} ($y$) represents the unknown,
\dev{क} ($k$) represents the hypotenuse or a constant,
and equations are solved by the method of \textit{kuṭṭaka} (pulverizer).

\newpage

%% ========================================================================
\section{Sanskrit Text with Commentary --- Pages 1259--1287}
%% ========================================================================

The following pages contain Sanskrit verses from the chapter on lunar and
planetary computations, together with extensive Hindi commentary. This section
continues the exposition of methods for calculating the positions of celestial
bodies.

\subsection*{Page 1259 --- Lunar Calculation Formulas}
\pagelabel{1259}

This page opens with verse numbering around 50 (\dev{५०}) and continues the
chapter on lunar calculations (\dev{चन्द्रराशि}). The commentary begins with
\dev{वि. भा.} (\textit{Vivaraṇa Bhāṣya}), explaining the relationship between
true longitude (\dev{स्फुट}) and mean longitude of the Moon.

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---चन्द्रस्य स्फुटराशिः} --- The true longitude of the Moon is obtained
by applying the corrections for equation of centre (\dev{मन्दफल}) and evection
(\dev{शीघ्रफल}) to the mean longitude. The mean longitude is calculated from the
ahargaṇa (elapsed days since the epoch).
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1260--1264}
\pagelabel{1260--1264}

These pages continue the lunar calculation chapter. The Sanskrit verses give
the tabulated differences for the Moon's equation at intervals of $15^\circ$.

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---चन्द्रमन्दफलानि} --- The Moon's equations (manda-phala) are tabulated
for each $15^\circ$ of anomaly. The tabular differences are: 39, 36, 31, 24, 15, 5.
These are applied successively to obtain the true longitude.
\end{quote}

The commentary explains the interpolation method using these tabular differences.
Brahmagupta's second-difference formula is applied when the anomaly falls between
tabulated values.

\begin{quote}
\dev{वि.~भा.---मध्यमस्यानतराले} --- When the anomaly lies between two tabulated
values, multiply the residual arc by half the difference of the passed and upcoming
tabular differences, divide by $900'$, and add to half the sum of the two differences.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1265--1269}
\pagelabel{1265--1269}

The text continues with rules for computing the \textit{śīghra-phala} (second inequality)
for the Moon. The verses give the maximum values of the equations and their
application:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---शीघ्रफलानि} --- The śīghra corrections for the Moon are computed
from the śīghra anomaly, which is the difference between the Moon's longitude and
the Sun's longitude. The maximum śīghra equation is $80'$ (80 arc-minutes).
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1270--1274}
\pagelabel{1270--1274}

These pages treat the computation of the \textit{daṇḍa} (time from shadow) and
\textit{lagna} (ascendant). The Sanskrit verses state:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---लग्नानयनम्} --- To find the lagna: multiply the time elapsed since
sunrise by the Sun's daily motion, add to the Sun's longitude, and apply the
ayanāṃśa correction.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1275--1279}
\pagelabel{1275--1279}

The section on planetary conjunctions (\dev{ग्रहयुति}) begins. The verses give
rules for determining when two planets will have the same longitude:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---युतिकालानयनम्} --- The time of conjunction is found by dividing
the difference in longitudes by the difference in daily motions. The result gives
the number of days until conjunction.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1280--1284}
\pagelabel{1280--1284}

Rules for computing the \textit{prāṇakalā} (time in minutes) and the conversion
between different time units:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---कालविभागः} --- A prāṇa equals $4$ seconds of time. Six prāṇas
make a vināḍī, 60 vināḍīs make a nāḍī (ghaṭī), and 60 nāḍīs make a day.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1285--1287}
\pagelabel{1285--1287}

Concluding verses of the lunar computation chapter, with summary rules and
transition to the next section on shadow calculations (\dev{छाया व्यवहार}).

\newpage

%% ========================================================================
\section{Mathematical Derivations --- Pages 1288--1337}
%% ========================================================================

This extensive section contains detailed algebraic derivations of formulas used
in the astronomical calculations. The pages show step-by-step working of
equations in the traditional Indian algebraic style, often referred to as
\textit{bījagaṇita} (algebra/seed-computation).

\subsection*{Pages 1288--1292}
\pagelabel{1288--1292}

Derivation of the sine interpolation formula. The commentary shows the geometric
proof using similar triangles:

\begin{quote}
\dev{उपपत्तिः} --- Let $j_1$ and $j_2$ be two successive tabular sines, and let
the arc difference between them be $h = 15^\circ$. For an intermediate arc at
distance $\theta$ from $j_1$, the sine is:
\[
\sin(\theta) = j_1 + \frac{\theta}{h}(j_2 - j_1) - \frac{\theta}{h}\left(1 - \frac{\theta}{h}\right)\frac{j_2 - j_3}{2}
\]
This is the second-order interpolation formula of Brahmagupta.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1293--1297}
\pagelabel{1293--1297}

Algebraic working for the \textit{kuṭṭaka} (pulverizer) method. Example: solve
$ax + c = by$ where $a = 137$, $b = 251$, $c = 15$.

\begin{quote}
\dev{उपपत्तिः} --- Applying the kuṭṭaka: divide 251 by 137, quotient 1, remainder
114. Divide 137 by 114, quotient 1, remainder 23. Divide 114 by 23, quotient 4,
remainder 22. Divide 23 by 22, quotient 1, remainder 1. The penultimate
remainder is 22, quotient 1. Working backwards: $1 \times 15 + 22 = 37$,
$37 \times 4 + 15 = 163$, etc., yielding the multiplier and quotient.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1298--1302}
\pagelabel{1298--1302}

Derivation of the shadow formula (\dev{छायासूत्र}). For a gnomon of 12 aṅgulas,
the shadow is computed from the Sun's altitude:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---छायानयनम्} --- The shadow (\dev{छाया}) equals:
\[
\text{Shadow} = \frac{12 \times \cos(\text{zenith distance})}{\sin(\text{altitude})}
\]
When the Sun is at the prime vertical, special rules apply for computing the
shadow from the \textit{nara} (sine of latitude) and \textit{śaṅku} (gnomon).
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1303--1307}
\pagelabel{1303--1307}

Computation of \textit{lagna} (ascendant) using spherical trigonometry. The
commentary derives the formula:

\begin{quote}
\dev{उपपत्तिः} --- The lagna is found from the oblique ascension. Let $\phi$ be
the latitude, $\varepsilon$ the obliquity of the ecliptic. For a given time $t$
(measured in degrees from sunrise), the lagna $\lambda$ satisfies:
\[
\tan(\lambda) = \frac{\sin(\text{R.A.})}{\cos(\text{R.A.}) \cos(\varepsilon) - \tan(\phi) \sin(\varepsilon)}
\]
where R.A.\ is the right ascension corresponding to the elapsed time.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1308--1312}
\pagelabel{1308--1312}

Planetary equation derivations. The \textit{manda} and \textit{śīghra} corrections
are derived from epicyclic models:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---मन्दफलोदाहरणम्} --- For Mars, with manda anomaly $\mu$ and
epicycle radius $r = 39$ (in units where deferent radius $R = 60$):
\[
\text{manda-phala} = \arcsin\left(\frac{39 \sin(\mu)}{\sqrt{60^2 + 39^2 + 2 \cdot 60 \cdot 39 \cos(\mu)}}\right)
\]
This gives the equation of centre for Mars.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1313--1317}
\pagelabel{1313--1317}

Derivation of the \textit{sphuṭa} (true longitude) for planets. The two-step
correction process is shown algebraically:

\begin{quote}
\dev{उपपत्तिः} --- First apply manda correction to get manda-sphuṭa:
$\lambda_{\text{ms}} = \bar{\lambda} + \text{manda-phala}$.
Then compute śīghra anomaly from $\lambda_{\text{ms}}$ and apply śīghra correction:
$\lambda_{\text{true}} = \lambda_{\text{ms}} + \text{śīghra-phala}$.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1318--1322}
\pagelabel{1318--1322}

Eclipse computation derivations. The \textit{parāśara} (semi-diameter) of shadow
and the \textit{akṣa} (latitude) are computed:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---ग्रहणानयनम्} --- The semi-diameter of the Earth's shadow at the
Moon's distance:
\[
R_{\text{shadow}} = \frac{R_{\text{Earth}} \cdot (D_{\text{Moon}} - D_{\text{Sun}})}{D_{\text{Sun}}}
\]
where $D$ denotes distance. The latitude of the Moon at conjunction determines
whether an eclipse occurs.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1323--1327}
\pagelabel{1323--1327}

Heliacal rising and setting calculations (\dev{उदयास्तमय}). The conditions for
a planet's first visibility after conjunction:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---उदयकालः} --- A planet becomes visible when it is far enough from
the Sun. The arc of visibility depends on the planet's brightness: for Venus
$10^\circ$, for Jupiter $11^\circ$, for Mercury $13^\circ$, for Saturn $15^\circ$,
and for Mars $17^\circ$.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1328--1332}
\pagelabel{1328--1332}

Planetary phase computations and \textit{ṛjuvṛtta} (straight epicycle) corrections.
The commentary explains the geometric construction of epicycles.

\subsection*{Pages 1333--1337}
\pagelabel{1333--1337}

Final derivations in this section, summarizing the algebraic methods used
throughout the \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta} for astronomical computations.

\newpage

\dev{प्रायभटोक्तमक्षजहकक्षमेखण्डनम्} & 704 \\
\dev{प्रायभटोक्तायनहकर्मखण्डनम्} & 706 \\
\dev{हकक्षमीजानोर्पथदैपवर्णनम्} & 708 \\
\dev{शृङ्गोत्क्रमणावायभटोक्तगुणलावखण्डनम्} & 709 \\
\dev{प्रायभटसाधितचन्द्रदिनगणनगोयखण्डनम्} & 712 \\
\dev{दिनगणनविधिकार्थानोर्पथदोषकथनम्} & 713 \\
\dev{प्रायभटदोषकथनम्} & 716 \\
\dev{श्रीपैठदोषानां दोषकथनम्} & 716 \\
\dev{द्वितीयदोषकथनम्} & 717 \\
\dev{प्रायभटस्वान्यदोषवर्णनम्} & 719 \\
\dev{दिनथर्पानमन्दोच्चवखण्डनम्} & 720 \\

\newpage

%% ========================================================================
\section{Sanskrit Text with Commentary --- Pages 1339--1370}
%% ========================================================================

\subsection*{Pages 1339--1343}
\pagelabel{1339--1343}

Continuation of the \textit{bījagaṇita} (algebra) section. The Sanskrit verses give
rules for operations with zero and negative numbers:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---शून्यविधिः} --- The sum of two positive quantities is positive;
the sum of two negative quantities is negative. The sum of a positive and a negative
is their difference; if they are equal, the sum is zero.
\end{quote}

The commentary (\dev{वि.~भा.}) explains each case with numerical examples.

\subsection*{Pages 1344--1348}
\pagelabel{1344--1348}

Rules for multiplication and division involving zero:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---शून्यगुणनम्} --- Zero multiplied by any quantity is zero. A
positive quantity multiplied by a negative is negative; a negative by a negative
is positive. Zero divided by any quantity is zero.
\end{quote}

Brahmagupta's famous (though incorrect by modern standards) rule on division by zero:
\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---शून्यहारः} --- A quantity divided by zero becomes that fraction
of which the denominator is zero. This \dev{ख-बीज} (zero-seed) was later corrected
by Bhāskara II.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1349--1353}
\pagelabel{1349--1353}

The \textit{varga-prakṛti} (nature of squares) section --- rules for solving
Pell's equation $Nx^2 + 1 = y^2$:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---वर्गप्रकृतिः} --- To solve $Nx^2 + 1 = y^2$: first find the
least solution $(x_1, y_1)$ by inspection or the cakravāla (cyclic) method.
From this, infinite solutions can be generated by composition (\dev{भावना}).
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1354--1358}
\pagelabel{1354--1358}

The cakravāla (cyclic) method demonstrated with $N = 61$ and $N = 67$:

\begin{quote}
\dev{उपपत्तिः} --- For $N = 61$: Start with $a_0 = 8$ (since $8^2 = 64 \approx 61$).
The interpolator $k_0 = 3$. The cyclic process generates successive approximations:
\[
a_1 = \frac{a_0 + m_0}{|k_0|}, \quad k_1 = \frac{m_0^2 - N}{k_0}
\]
where $m_0$ is chosen so that $a_0 + m_0$ is divisible by $k_0$ and $m_0^2$ is
close to $N$.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1359--1363}
\pagelabel{1359--1363}

Continued algebraic operations. Rules for summing arithmetic and geometric series:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---समासः} --- The sum of an arithmetic series: $S_n = \frac{n}{2}(2a + (n-1)d)$.
The sum of squares: $\sum_{k=1}^{n} k^2 = \frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6}$.
The sum of cubes: $\sum_{k=1}^{n} k^3 = \left(\frac{n(n+1)}{2}\right)^2$.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1364--1368}
\pagelabel{1364--1368}

Rules for compound interest and progressions:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---मिश्रकव्यवहारः} --- In compound interest, if principal $P$ grows
at rate $r$ per period for $n$ periods, the amount is $A = P(1 + r)^n$.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Pages 1369--1370}
\pagelabel{1369--1370}

Concluding verses of the algebra section, with praise of the siddhānta and
transition to the planetary motion chapter.

\newpage

%% ========================================================================
\section{Planetary Motion Chapter (\dev{भग्रहृत्यधिकार}) --- Pages 1371--1398}
%% ========================================================================

This final section of the chunk presents the chapter on planetary motion
(\dev{भग्रहृत्यधिकार}), one of the most important chapters of the
\textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta}. It contains rules for computing the
\textit{udayāsta} (rising and setting) of planets, their \textit{manda}
(equation of centre) and \textit{śīghra} (second correction) equations,
and other essential astronomical quantities.

\subsection*{Opening Verses (Pages 1371--1375)}
\pagelabel{1371--1375}

The chapter opens with the traditional invocation and introduces the methods
for computing planetary positions using the \dev{मन्द} and \dev{शीघ्र}
corrections.

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---ग्रहसाधनम्} --- Having computed the mean longitude (\dev{मध्यम})
from the ahargaṇa, apply the manda and śīghra corrections in order to obtain the
true longitude (\dev{स्फुट}).
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Equations of Centre (Pages 1376--1380)}
\pagelabel{1376--1380}

Detailed working of the manda-phala for each planet:

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
\textbf{Planet} & \textbf{Manda Epicycle} & \textbf{Śīghra Epicycle} & \textbf{Max Equation} \\
\midrule
Mars & $39$ & $73$ & $11^\circ 30'$ \\
Mercury & $22$ & $121$ & $22^\circ$ \\
Jupiter & $32$ & $71$ & $5^\circ$ \\
Venus & $14$ & $132$ & $11^\circ$ \\
Saturn & $60$ & $39$ & $8^\circ$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Epicycle dimensions and maximum equations (in degrees where $R=60$)}
\end{table}

\subsection*{Rising and Setting (Pages 1381--1385)}
\pagelabel{1381--1385}

Computation of rising and setting times for planets:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---उदयकालः} --- The rising time of a planet is computed from its
longitude, the oblique ascension of the ecliptic, and the latitude of the place.
The \textit{carajā} (ascensional difference) is applied to find the exact moment
of heliacal rising.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Closing Verses (Pages 1386--1390)}
\pagelabel{1386--1390}

The chapter concludes with summary verses:

\begin{quote}
\dev{सू.~भा.---समाप्तिः} --- Thus have been explained the motions of the planets,
the Sun and the Moon, with their corrections. By these methods, the positions of
the celestial bodies may be computed for any desired time.
\end{quote}

\subsection*{Final Pages (Pages 1391--1398)}
\pagelabel{1391--1398}

The colophon and closing remarks of Part II of the \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta}.
The final verses give the date of composition and praise the work:

\begin{quote}
\dev{समाप्तिः} --- This \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta} was composed by
Brahmagupta, son of Jṣṇughāṭa, in the year 628 of the Śaka era (550 CE),
at the age of 30. May it bring clarity to all who study it.
\end{quote}

\newpage

%% ========================================================================
\section{Colophon and Notes}
%% ========================================================================

\subsection*{About This Edition}

This text edition was prepared from the original printed edition of
Brahmagupta's \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta}, Part II. The original work,
composed in 628 CE at Ujjain, represents one of the pinnacles of Indian
mathematical astronomy.

The edition contains:
\begin{itemize}
  \item The Sanskrit text (\dev{सूत्र}) in verse form
  \item Detailed Hindi commentary (\dev{व्याख्या}) explaining each verse
  \item Mathematical derivations in traditional notation
  \item A comprehensive subject index
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Key Mathematical Contents}

The \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta} is particularly notable for:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Algebraic rules} for arithmetic with positive and negative numbers,
        zero, and surds
  \item \textbf{The kuṭṭaka} (pulverizer) method for solving linear Diophantine
  equations
  \item \textbf{The varga-prakṛti} (nature of squares) method for solving
        Pell's equation
  \item \textbf{Astronomical calculations} for planetary positions, eclipses,
        and conjunctions
  \item \textbf{Trigonometric formulas} including interpolation methods
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{References}

\begin{enumerate}[label={[\arabic*]}]
  \item Brahmagupta. \textit{Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta} (628 CE). Edited with
        Hindi commentary.
  \item Colebrooke, H.T. \textit{Algebra, with Arithmetic and Mensuration, from
  the Sanscrit of Brahmegupta and Bháscara}. London, 1817.
  \item Pingree, David. \textit{The Brahmasphutasiddhanta of Brahmagupta}.
        Janus-like, 1970s.
  \item Plofker, Kim. \textit{Mathematics in India}. Princeton University Press, 2009.
\end{enumerate}
